be the edges implied by
Lemma

48
and
Lemma

57
.
By
Lemma

55
, we know that
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
are only adjacent to nodes of
P
in
S
.
Furthermore, neither
v_{2}^{3}
nor
v_{4}^{3}
has an edge to a node not on
P
, as then we can make progress according to
Lemma

56
.
Hence, in the remaining case, assume
v_{1}^{3}
or
v_{3}^{3}
has an edge
e_{3b}
to a node
z_{b}
.
Let
v_{1}^{b}-v_{2}^{b}-v_{3}^{b}-v_{4}^{b}-v_{1}^{b}
be the
4
-cycle if
z_{b}
is a
\mathcal{C}_{4}
, and otherwise those 4 vertices are simply
4
distinct vertices of
z_{b}
.
Assume w.l.o.g. that
e_{3b}
is incident to
v_{1}^{b}
.

By the
3
-Matching Lemma, either
v_{2}^{b}
or
v_{4}^{b}
has an edge
e
to a node
z^{\prime}\neq z_{b}
of
S
.
If
z^{\prime}
is not on
P
, then we can extend the gluing path and obtain
P^{\prime}=z_{k}-...-z_{4}-z_{1}-z_{3}-z_{b}-z
using the edge
e_{14}
and
e_{13}
.
If
z^{\prime}\in\{z_{1},z_{2}\}
then
P_{13}
and
e_{13}
together with
e_{3b}
and
e
form a double
(4,0)
-merge and hence, by
Lemma

39
, we can make progress.
If
z^{\prime}
is a node of
P
such that
i\geq 5
, then
P_{13}
and
e_{13}
together with
e_{3b}
,
e
and
P_{3i}
forms a double
(i+1,j)
-merge for some
j\leq 2
. Hence, by
Lemma

39
, we can make progress.
If
z^{\prime}
is
z_{4}
, consider the following two cases:
Either
e
is incident to
v_{2}^{4}
or
v_{4}^{4}
, then we can extend the gluing path to
P^{\prime}=z_{k}-...-z_{4}-z_{b}-z_{3}-z_{2}-z_{1}
using the edge
e
and
e_{3b}
incident to
z_{b}
.
If
e
is incident to
v_{3}^{4}
or
v_{1}^{4}
, then
P_{13}
and
e_{13}
together with
P_{34}
,
e_{3b}
and
e
forms a double
(5,1)
-merge. Hence, by
Lemma

39
, we can make progress.

Therefore,
z^{\prime}=z_{3}
, i.e.,
e
is incident to
z_{3}
.
Observe that if
e
is incident to either
v_{2}^{3}
or
v_{4}^{3}
, we can make progress according to
Lemma

56
.
Hence,
e
is incident to either
v_{1}^{3}
or
v_{3}^{3}
.
Moreover,
z_{b}
must have an edge
f
to some other node of
S
which is not
z_{3}
, otherwise
z_{b}
is not in
S
.
Now observe that we can interchange the roles of
e
and
e_{3b}
.
Hence, we can apply the same case distinction for
f
as for
e
and observe that in any case we make progress.
This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Hence, from now on we assume that in
G
the vertices of nodes
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
all cannot have an edge to a node
z
that is not on
P
.

Lemma 59
.

Let
P=z_{k}-z_{k-1}-...-z_{3}-z_{2}-z_{1}
be a gluing path of length
k\geq 4
such that in
G
the vertices of nodes
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
are all only adjacent to vertices of nodes of
P
.
Then we can make progress.

Proof.

Let
e_{13}
and
e_{14}
be the edges implied by
Lemma

48
and
Lemma

57
.
If
z_{1}
is incident to an edge
e
incident to some node
z_{i}
for
i\geq 5
, we can make progress as
P_{1i}
together with
e
forms a single
(i,j)
-merge for some
j\leq 2
.
If
z_{2}
is incident to an edge
e
incident to some node
z_{i}
for
i\geq 5
, we can make progress as
P_{1i}
together with
e
and
e_{13}
forms a double
(i,j)
-merge for some
j\leq 1
.
Hence, in both cases we can make progress according to
Lemma

39
.
Thus,
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
are only adjacent to
z_{3}
and
z_{4}
in
S
.

Next, assume
z_{3}
is incident to an edge
e
incident to some node
z_{i}
for
i\geq 5
.
If
e
is incident to either
v_{2}^{3}
or
v_{4}^{3}
, then
P_{13}
and
e_{13}
together with
P_{4i}
,
e
and
e_{14}
form a double
(i,j)
-merge for some
j\leq 1
.
If
e
is incident to either
v_{1}^{3}
or
v_{3}^{3}
, then
P_{4i},e,e_{13},e_{14}
forms a single
(i-1,j)
-merge for some
j\leq 1
.
Hence, in both cases we can make progress according to
Lemma

39
.

Now observe that we can make progress if one of
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
is connected to some
z_{i},i\geq 5
.
Hence, assume that this is not the case.
Since
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
have no edge to a node of
S
not on
P
, this implies that
z_{4}
is a cut node.
But then
z_{5}
and
z_{3},z_{2},z_{1}
are not in the same segment
S
, a contradiction.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Finally, using the above lemmas we can easily prove
Lemma

46
, which in turn proves
Lemma

16
.

Proof of
Lemma

46
.

By
Lemma

51
we can compute in polynomial time a gluing path
P=z_{k}-z_{k-1}-...-z_{2}-z_{1}
of length
k-1\geq 4
, unless we can make progress.

Lemma

58
implies that the nodes
z_{1}
,
z_{2}
, and
z_{3}
all have no edge to a node of
S
that is not on
P
, unless we can make progress.
Now
Lemma

59
implies that we can make progress, which proves the lemma.
∎

Appendix

Appendix A
Reduction to Structured Graphs

This section is dedicated to the proof of
Lemma

3
, which we first restate.

See
3

We assume throughout this section that
\alpha\in[\nicefrac{{6}}{{5}},\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{100}}]
.
Let
\mathsf{ALG}
be a polynomial-time algorithm that, given an
(\alpha,\varepsilon)
-structured graph
G^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime})
, returns an
\alpha
-approximate 2EC spanning subgraph
\mathsf{ALG}(G^{\prime})\subseteq E^{\prime}
of
G^{\prime}
.
In the following, we define a reduction algorithm which recursively modifies and partitions the input graph
G
until we obtain
(\alpha,\varepsilon)
-structured graphs, then solves these with
\mathsf{ALG}
, and finally puts the resulting solutions together to an overall solution for
G
.
In particular, our reduction checks for structures that are forbidden in
(\alpha,\varepsilon)
-structured graphs. These are graphs of constant size (w.r.t.
\varepsilon
),
parallel edges and self loops,
1-vertex cuts, small
\alpha
-contractible subgraphs (w.r.t.
\varepsilon
), irrelevant edges, non-isolating 2-vertex cuts, large 3-vertex cuts, large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts for
k\in\{4,\ldots,8\}
, and large
4
-vertex cuts (cf.
Definition

1
). We first restate definitions of some of these structures from
[
GGJ23
]
, and then formally define
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts and large
4
-vertex cuts.

Definition 18

(
\alpha
-contractible subgraph
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Let
\alpha\geq 1
be a fixed constant. A 2EC subgraph
C
of a 2EC graph
G
is
\alpha
-
contractible
if every 2ECSS of
G
contains at least
\frac{1}{\alpha}\lvert E(C)\rvert
edges with both endpoints in
V(C)
.

Definition 19

(irrelevant edge
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Given a graph
G
and an edge
e=uv\in E(G)
, we say that
e
is
irrelevant
if
\{u,v\}
is a 2-vertex cut of
G
.

Definition 20

(non-isolating 2-vertex cut
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Given a graph
G
, and a 2-vertex cut
\{u,v\}
of
G
, we say that
\{u,v\}
is
isolating
if
G\setminus\{u,v\}
has exactly two connected components, one of which is of size 1.
Otherwise, it is
non-isolating
.

Definition 21

(large 3-vertex cuts
[
GHL24
]
)
.

Given a graph
G
and a 3-vertex cut
\{u,v,w\}
of
G
, we say that
\{u,v,w\}
is
large
if there is a partition
(V_{1},V_{2})
of
V\setminus\{u,v,w\}
with
E[V_{1},V_{2}]=\emptyset

such that
|V_{1}|,|V_{2}|\geq\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil-1
.
Otherwise, we call it
small
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{k}
be a cycle on
k
vertices
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
in a graph
G
.
We say that
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
is a
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cut
if
G\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
has strictly more connected components than
G
.

Definition 22

(large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts)
.

Let
k\in\{4,5,6,7,8\}
.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a graph and
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
be a
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cut of
G
. We call
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}

large
if there is a partition
(V_{1},V_{2})
of
V\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
with
E[V_{1},V_{2}]=\emptyset
and
|V_{1}|,|V_{2}|\geq\lceil\frac{k}{\alpha-1}\rceil-1
.
Otherwise, we call it
small
.

Definition 23

(large
4
-vertex cuts)
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a graph and
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
be a
4
-vertex cut of
G
.
We call
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}

large
if

1.

there is a partition
(V_{1},V_{2})
of
V\setminus\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
with
E[V_{1},V_{2}]=\emptyset
and
\lceil\frac{6}{\alpha-1}\rceil-1\leq|V_{1}|,|V_{2}|
, or

2.

there is a partition
(V_{1},V_{2})
of
V\setminus\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
with
E[V_{1},V_{2}]=\emptyset
,
G_{i}\coloneq G[V_{i}\cup\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}]
and
G^{\prime}_{i}:=G_{i}|\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, such that
for any 2ECSS
H_{2}
for
G^{\prime}_{2}

there exists
a set of edges
Z\subseteq E

such that (i)
|Z|\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})
and (ii)
H_{2}\cup Z
is a 2ECSS of
G
.

Otherwise, we call it
small
.

For all structures except large 3-vertex cuts, large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts, and large
k
-vertex cuts, we can use the reduction presented in
[
GGJ23
]
. The main contribution of this section is to show how to reduce to graphs without large 3-vertex cuts, large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts and large
k
-vertex cuts, given that all other substructures have already been removed. For large 3-vertex cuts, we mainly follows the approach of
[
GHL24
]
but parameterize the required size of the cut by
\alpha
instead of assuming
\alpha\geq\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}
as in
[
GHL24
]
.

This section is structured as follows. In
Section

A.1
we first introduce definitions to classify solutions regarding 2-vertex cuts and 3-vertex cuts, and then give the formal description of the reduction algorithm, which is split into four algorithm environments, where
Algorithm

1
is the main algorithm, which calls
Algorithms

2
,
3
,
4
and
5
as subroutines.
In
Section

A.2
, we give auxiliary results, mainly regarding the removal of 2-vertex cuts, 3-vertex cuts, large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cuts, and large
4
-vertex cuts. Finally, in
Section

A.3
we combine these auxiliary results together and give the main proof of
Lemma

3
.

A.1
Definitions and the Algorithm

We will heavily use the following two facts on 2EC graphs with contracted components. We will not refer to them explicitly.

Fact 60
.

Let
G
be a 2EC graph and
W\subseteq V(G)
. Then
G|W
is 2EC.

Fact 61
.

Let
H
be a 2EC subgraph of a 2EC graph
G
, and
S
and
S^{\prime}
be 2EC spanning subgraphs of
H
and
G|H
, respectively. Then
S\cup S^{\prime}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, where we interpret
S^{\prime}
as edges of
G
after decontracting
V(H)
.

A.1.1
Definitions for Non-Isolating 2-Vertex Cuts

Let
\{u,v\}
be a
2
-vertex cut in a graph
G
.
Note that that we can find a partition
(V_{1},V_{2})
of
V\setminus\{u,v\}
such that
V_{1}\neq\emptyset\neq V_{2}
and there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
. Once we delete
\{u,v\}
from
G
, the graph breaks into various components such that each is either part of
V_{1}
or
V_{2}
. Similarly,
\mathsf{OPT}(G)
breaks into various components once we remove
\{u,v\}
such that each component is either part of
V_{1}
or
V_{2}
. In the following definition, we consider different types of graphs that match
H\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}(G)[V_{1}\cup\{u,v\}]
(or analogously
\mathsf{OPT}(G)[V_{2}\cup\{u,v\}]
).

Definition 24

(2-vertex cut solution types)
.

Let
H
be a graph and
u,v\in V(H)
. Let
H^{\prime}
be obtained by contracting each 2EC component
C
of
H
into a single super-node
C
, and let
C(x)
be the corresponding super-node of the 2EC component of
H
that contains
x\in V(H)
. We define the following types of
H^{\prime}
with respect to
\{u,v\}
:

Type
\mathsf{A}
:

H^{\prime}
is composed of a single super-node
H^{\prime}=C(u)=C(v)
.

Type
\mathsf{B}
:

H^{\prime}
is a
C(u)-C(v)
path of length at least
1
.

Type
\mathsf{C}
:

H^{\prime}
consists of two isolated super-nodes
C(u)
and
C(v)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{2}\coloneq\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{C}\}
be the set of 2-vertex cut optimal solution types.

Definition 25

(Order on types)
.

\mathsf{A}\succ\mathsf{B}\succ\mathsf{C}
.

A.1.2
Definitions for Large 3-Vertex Cuts

Similarly to 2-vertex cuts and
Definition

24
, we give in the following definition types that match the subgraph of an optimal solution that is induced by the partition corresponding to a large
3
-vertex cut
\{u,v,w\}
in
G
. Naturally, here are more patterns that can occur compared to 2-vertex cuts.

Definition 26

(3-vertex cut solution types)
.

Let
H
be a graph and
u,v,w\in V(H)
. Let
H^{\prime}
be obtained by contracting each 2EC component
C
of
H
into a single super-node
C
, and let
C(x)
be the corresponding super-node of the 2EC component of
H
that contains
x\in V(H)
. We define the following types of
H^{\prime}
with respect to
\{u,v,w\}
:

Type
\mathsf{A}
:

H^{\prime}
is composed of a single super-node
H^{\prime}=C(u)=C(v)=C(w)
.

Type
\mathsf{B1}
:

H^{\prime}
is a
C_{1}-C_{k}
path of length at least
1
such that
u,v,w\in C_{1}\cup C_{k}
and
\lvert\{u,v,w\}\cap C_{i}\rvert\leq 2
for
i\in\{1,k\}
. We assume w.l.o.g. that
C_{1}=C(u)=C(v)
.

Type
\mathsf{B2}
:

H^{\prime}
is composed of two isolated super-nodes
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
, where
\lvert\{u,v,w\}\cap C_{i}\rvert\leq 2
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. We assume w.l.o.g. that
C_{1}=C(u)=C(v)
.

Type
\mathsf{C1}
:

H^{\prime}
is a tree and
u
,
v
, and
w
are in distinct super-nodes.

Type
\mathsf{C2}
:

H^{\prime}
is composed of a
C_{1}-C_{k}
path of length at least
1
and an isolated super-node
C_{\ell}
such that
\lvert C_{i}\cap\{u,v,w\}\rvert=1
for
i\in\{1,k,\ell\}
.

Type
\mathsf{C3}
:

H^{\prime}
is composed of the three isolated super-nodes
C(u),C(v)
and
C(w)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{3}\coloneq\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2},\mathsf{C1},\mathsf{C2},\mathsf{C3}\}
be the set of 3-vertex cut optimal solution types.

Definition 27

(Order on types)
.

\mathsf{A}\succ\mathsf{B1}\succ\mathsf{B2}\succ\mathsf{C1}\succ\mathsf{C2}\succ\mathsf{C3}
.

The following proposition lists compatible solution types of both sides of a 3-vertex cut
\{u,v,w\}
. The proof is straightforward and, thus, omitted.
For example, if
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
(using the notation introduced below) is of type
\mathsf{C3}
, it is composed of three distinct 2EC components
C(u)
,
C(v)
, and
C(w)
. Therefore,
u
,
v
, and
w
must be in the same 2EC connected component in
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
as otherwise
\mathsf{OPT}
cannot be a 2ECSS, and thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
\mathsf{A}
.

Proposition 62

(feasible type combinations)
.

Let
\{u,v,w\}
be a large 3-vertex cut in a graph
G
, and
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a partition of
V\setminus\{u,v,w\}
such that
\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil-1\leq\lvert V_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert V_{2}\rvert
and there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
in
G
.
Let
G_{1}=G[V_{1}\cup\{u,v,w\}]
,
G_{2}=G[V_{2}\cup\{u,v,w\}]\setminus\{uv,vw,uw\}
and, for a fixed optimal solution
\mathsf{OPT}(G)
, let
\mathsf{OPT}_{i}=\mathsf{OPT}(G)\cap E(G_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then, with respect to
\{u,v,w\}
, if

(a)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
t\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2},\mathsf{C1},\mathsf{C2},\mathsf{C3}\}=\mathcal{T}_{3}
.

(b)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{B1}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
t\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2},\mathsf{C1},\mathsf{C2}\}
.

(c)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{B2}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
t\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2}\}
.

(d)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{C1}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
t\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{C1}\}
.

(e)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{C2}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
t\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1}\}
.

(f)

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{C3}
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
must be of type
\mathsf{A}
.

Observe that according to the above definition we have
\lvert\mathsf{OPT}(G)\rvert=\lvert\mathsf{OPT}_{1}\rvert+\lvert\mathsf{OPT}_{2}\rvert
.

A.1.3
The Full Algorithm

We are now ready to state our main algorithm
\mathsf{RED}
(
Algorithm

1
).
Let
\mathsf{red}(G)\coloneq|\mathsf{RED}(G)|
denote the size of the solution computed by
\mathsf{RED}
for a given 2ECSS instance
G
.
The reductions applied in
Algorithms

1
,
1
,
1
,
1
and
1
(here and in the following, we do not distinguish between the line where the actual reduction is computed and the line in which the condition for the reduction is checked) are identical to the reductions given in
[
GGJ23
]
.
Specifically,
Algorithm

2
, which is called in
Algorithm

1
, reduces non-isolating 2-vertex cuts and was first given in
[
GGJ23
]
. We emphasize that we present it slightly different, but without changing its actual behavior.
When
Algorithm

1
reaches
Algorithm

1
it calls
Algorithm

3
to handle a large 3-vertex cut
\{u,v,w\}

[
GHL24
]
, when it reaches
Algorithm

1
it calls
Algorithm

4
to handle a large
\mathcal{C}_{k}
cut
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
for
k\in\{4,5,6,7,8\}
(that is, it checks for
k=4
, then for
k=5
, and so on), and when it reaches
Algorithm

1
it calls
Algorithm

5
to handle a large
4
-vertex cut
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
.

Whenever the reduction returns edges
K
of contracted graphs
G|H
, i.e.,
K=\mathsf{RED}(G|H)
, we interpret these as the corresponding edges after decontracting
H
, e.g., in
Algorithm

1
in
Algorithm

1
or in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
.

Hence, from now on let us focus on
Algorithm

3
.
In
Section

A.2.2
we prove that the claimed sets of edges
F
in
Algorithm

3
exist and are of constant size.
The computations done in
Algorithm

3
(and in
Algorithm

2
in
Algorithm

2
) can be done as explained in
[
GGJ23
]
.
That is, we first compute via enumeration minimum-size subgraphs
\overline{\mathsf{OPT}}_{1}^{t}
of
G_{1}
of every type
t\in\mathcal{T}_{3}
if such a solution exists, because
G_{1}
is of constant size in this case.
If
\overline{\mathsf{OPT}}_{1}^{t}
does not exist,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{t}
also cannot exist.
Otherwise, that is,
\overline{\mathsf{OPT}}_{1}^{t}
exists, we check whether
G_{2}
is a graph that admits a solution that is compatible with
\overline{\mathsf{OPT}}_{1}^{t}
according to
Proposition

62
, and, if that is the case, we set
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{t}\coloneq\overline{\mathsf{OPT}}_{1}^{t}
.

Finally, note that if the algorithm reaches
Algorithm

1
in
Algorithm

1
, then
G
must be
(\alpha,\varepsilon)
-structured.
To show this, we will verify the following invariant via induction (
Lemma

102
) for all 2ECSS instances
G
:

\mathsf{red}(G)\leq\begin{cases}\mathsf{opt}(G)&\quad\text{if }\lvert V(G)\rvert\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\ ,\text{ and}\\
\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)&\quad\text{if }\lvert V(G)\rvert>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\ ,\end{cases}

(1)

where
f(G)\coloneq 4\varepsilon\cdot\lvert V(G)\rvert-16
.

Input:

A.2
Auxiliary Lemmas

The following two lemmas are standard results, and can be found in, e.g.,
[
HVV19
,
GGJ23
]
.

Lemma 63
.

Let
G
be a 2EC graph,
v
be a cut vertex of
G
, and
(V_{1},V_{2}),V_{1}\neq\emptyset\neq V_{2}
, be a partition of
V\setminus\{v\}
such that there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
. Then,
\mathsf{opt}(G)=\mathsf{opt}(G[V_{1}\cup\{v\}])+\mathsf{opt}(G[V_{2}\cup\{v\}])
.

Lemma 64
.

Let
G
be a 2VC multigraph with
|V(G)|\geq 3
. Then, there exists a minimum size 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
that is simple, that is, it contains no self loops or parallel edges.

Next, we replicate a lemma of
[
GGJ23
]
that
shows that irrelevant edges are irrelevant for obtaining an optimal 2ECSS.

Lemma 65

(Lemma 2.1 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Let
e=uv
be an irrelevant edge of a 2VC simple graph
G=(V,E)
. Then there exists a minimum-size 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
not containing
e
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that all optimal 2EC spanning subgraphs of
G
contain
e
, and let
\mathsf{OPT}
be one such solution. Define
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}\setminus\{e\}
.
Clearly,
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}
cannot be 2EC as it would contradict the optimality of
\mathsf{OPT}
. Let
(V_{1},V_{2})
be any partition of
V\setminus\{u,v\}
,
V_{1}\neq\emptyset\neq V_{2}
, such that there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, which must exist since
\{u,v\}
is a 2-vertex cut.
Notice that at least one of
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}[V_{1}\cup\{u,v\}]
and
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{2}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}[V_{2}\cup\{u,v\}]
, say
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{2}
, needs to be connected, as otherwise
\mathsf{OPT}
would not be 2EC.
We also have that
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}
is disconnected, as otherwise
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}
would be 2EC.
More precisely,
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}
consists of exactly two connected components
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(u)
and
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(v)
containing
u
and
v
, respectively. Assume w.l.o.g. that
|V(\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(u))|\geq 2
.
Observe that there must exist an edge
f
between
V(\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(u))
and
V(\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(v))
. Otherwise,
u
would be a 1-vertex cut separating
V(\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(u))\setminus\{u\}
from
V\setminus(V(\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}_{1}(u))\cup\{u\})
.
Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime\prime}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}^{\prime}\cup\{f\}
is an optimal 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
not containing
e
, a contradiction.
∎

The next lemma shows that for both, 3-vertex cuts and 2-vertex cuts, type
\mathsf{A}
solutions are more expensive than the cheapest solution.

Lemma 66
.

If
Algorithm

2
(
Algorithm

3
) reaches
Algorithm

2
(
Algorithm

3
) and
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
exists, then
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+1
.

Proof.

If
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
exists and
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}
then
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
is 2EC and
\alpha
-contractible.
However, since we assume that we reached
Algorithm

2
(
Algorithm

3
), we have that
|V_{1}|\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
. This is a contradiction, because we would have contracted
G_{1}
in
Algorithm

1
of
Algorithm

1
earlier. Thus, it must be that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+1
. ∎

A.2.1
Auxiliary Lemmas for
Algorithm

2

Proposition 67
.

If
Algorithm

1
executes
Algorithm

2
, then
G
contains no self loops, parallel edges, irrelevant edges, or
\alpha
-contractible subgraphs with at most
\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
vertices.

Lemma 68

(Lemma A.4 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Let
G
be the graph when
Algorithm

1
executes
Algorithm

2
,
\{u,v\}
be a non-isolating 2-vertex cut of
G
,
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a partition of
V\setminus\{u,v\}
such that
V_{1}\neq\emptyset\neq V_{2}
and there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, and
H
be a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. Let
G_{i}=G[V_{i}\cup\{u,v\}]
and
H_{i}=E(G_{i})\cap H
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. The following statements are true.

1.

Both
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are of a type in
\mathcal{T}_{2}
with respect to
\{u,v\}
. Furthermore, if one of the two is of type
\mathsf{C}
, then the other must be of type
\mathsf{A}
.

2.

If
H_{i}
, for an
i\in\{1,2\}
, is of type
\mathsf{C}
, then there exists one edge
f\in E(G_{i})
such that
H_{i}^{\prime}\coloneq H_{i}\cup\{f\}
is of type
\mathsf{B}
. As a consequence, there exists a 2EC spanning subgraph
H^{\prime}
where
H_{i}^{\prime}\coloneq H^{\prime}\cap E(G_{i})
is of type
\mathsf{A}
or
\mathsf{B}
.

Proof.

First note that
uv\notin E(G)
since there are no irrelevant edges by
Proposition

67
.
We first prove the first claim. We prove it for
H_{1}
, the other case being symmetric.
First notice that if
H_{1}
contains a connected component not containing
u
nor
v
,
H
would be disconnected. Hence,
H_{1}
consists of one connected component or two connected components
H_{1}(u)
and
H_{1}(v)
containing
u
and
v
, respectively.

Suppose first that
H_{1}
consists of one connected component. Let us contract the 2EC components of
H_{1}
, hence obtaining a tree
T
. If
T
consists of a single vertex,
H_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
. Otherwise, consider the path
P
(possibly of length
0
)
between the super nodes resulting from the contraction of the 2EC components
C(u)
and
C(v)
containing
u
and
v
, respectively. Assume by contradiction that
T
contains an edge
e
not in
P
. Then
e
does not belong to any cycle of
H
, contradicting the fact that
H
is 2EC. Thus,
H_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{B}
(in particular
P
has length at least 1 since
H_{1}
is not 2EC).

Assume now that
H_{1}
consists of 2 connected components
H_{1}(u)
and
H_{1}(v)
. Let
T_{u}
and
T_{v}
be the two trees obtained by contracting the 2EC components of
H_{1}(u)
and
H_{1}(v)
, respectively. By the same argument as before, the 2-edge connectivity of
H
implies that these two trees contain no edge. Hence,
H_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{C}
.

The second part of the first claim follows easily since if
H_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{C}
and
H_{2}
is not of type
\mathsf{A}
, then
H
would either be disconnected or it would contain at least one bridge edge, because
uv\notin E(G)
.

We now move to the second claim of the lemma. There must exist an edge
f
in
G_{i}
between the two connected components of
H_{i}
since otherwise at least one of
u
or
v
would be a cut vertex.
Clearly,
H^{\prime}_{i}\coloneq H_{i}\cup\{f\}
satisfies the claim. For the second part of the claim, consider any 2EC spanning
subgraph
H^{\prime}
and let
H^{\prime}_{i}\coloneq H^{\prime}\cap E(G_{i})
.
The claim holds if there exists one such
H^{\prime}
where
H^{\prime}_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
or
\mathsf{B}
, hence assume that this is not the case. Hence,
H^{\prime}_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{C}
by the first part of this lemma. The same argument as above implies the existence of an edge
f
in
G_{i}
such that
H^{\prime\prime}_{i}\coloneq H^{\prime}_{i}\cup\{f\}
is of type
\mathsf{B}
, implying the claim for
H^{\prime\prime}\coloneq H^{\prime}\cup\{f\}
.
∎

Lemma 69
.

In
Algorithm

2
it holds that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}\geq 3
.

Proof.

Consider any feasible 2ECSS solution
H_{1}
for
G_{1}
, and let
H^{\prime}_{1}=H_{1}\setminus\{u,v\}
be the corresponding solution for
G_{1}|\{u,v\}
.
Since
G_{1}|\{u,v\}
contains at least
3
vertices and
H^{\prime}_{1}
is a feasible 2ECSS solution,
H^{\prime}_{1}
must contain at least
3
edges.
Thus,
|H_{1}|\geq|H^{\prime}_{1}|\geq 3
.
∎

We have an immediate corollary from
Lemma

66
and
\alpha>1
.

Corollary 70
.

If
Algorithm

2
reaches
Algorithm

2
and
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
exists, then
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+2
. In particular,
t^{\min}\neq\mathsf{A}
.

Lemma 71

(Claim 1 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

If
Algorithm

2
is executed on instance
G
, there exists an optimal solution
\mathsf{opt}(G)
such that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{B}
or
\mathsf{C}
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction, consider any optimal solution
\mathsf{OPT}(G)
and assume that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
.

Lemma

68
implies that every feasible solution must use at least
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}
edges from
G_{1}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}>\alpha\cdot\min\{\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}},\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}\}
by
Lemma

66
.
If
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}+1
, then we obtain an alternative optimum solution with the desired property by taking
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
, and, in case
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
is of type
\mathsf{C}
, by adding one edge
f
between the connected components of
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
whose existence is guaranteed by
Lemma

68
. Otherwise, that is,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}
, we must have
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}>\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}
. Specifically,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}+1\geq 4
by
Lemma

69
.
Lemma

68
also implies
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}+1
, which together gives
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}+1
. Then,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}>\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}=\alpha(\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}-1)
implies
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\leq 5
since
\alpha\geq\frac{6}{5}
. We established that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\in\{4,5\}
.

If
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}=4
,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
has to be a
4
-cycle
C=u-a-v-b-u
. Notice that the edge
ab
cannot exist since otherwise
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}=3
due to the path
u-a-b-v
. Then every feasible solution must include the 4 edges of
C
to guarantee degree at least 2 on
a
and
b
. But this makes
C
an
\alpha
-contractible subgraph on
4\leq\frac{4}{\varepsilon}
vertices, which is a contradiction to
Proposition

67
.

If
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}=5
, the minimality of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
implies that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
is a
5
-cycle, say
C=u-a-v-b-c-u
. Observe that the edge
ab
cannot exist, since otherwise
\{va,ab,bc,cu\}
would be a type
\mathsf{B}
solution in
G_{1}
of size
4
, contradicting
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}=5
. A symmetric construction shows that edge
ac
cannot exist. Hence, every feasible solution restricted to
G_{1}
must include the edges
\{au,av\}
and furthermore at least
3
more edge incident to
b
and
c
, so at least
5
edges altogether. This implies that
C
is an
\alpha
-constractible subgraph of size
5\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
vertices, which is a contradiction to
Proposition

67
.
∎

Lemma 72

(Part of the proof of Lemma A.6 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

2
in
Algorithm

2
applies and both,
H^{\prime}_{1}
and
H^{\prime}_{2}
are 2EC spanning subgraphs of
G^{\prime}_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively, then there exist edges
F^{\prime}
with
|F^{\prime}|\leq 2
such that
H^{\prime}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

First note that
H^{\prime}_{i}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, must be of type
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{B}
, or
\mathsf{C}
. If one of them is of type
\mathsf{A}
or they are both of type
\mathsf{B}
, then
H^{\prime}_{1}\cup H^{\prime}_{2}
is 2EC.
If one of them is of type
\mathsf{B}
, say
H^{\prime}_{1}
, and the other of type
\mathsf{C}
, say
H^{\prime}_{2}
, then let
H^{\prime}_{2}(u)
and
H^{\prime}_{2}(v)
be the two 2EC components of
H^{\prime}_{2}
containing
u
and
v
, respectively. By
Lemma

68
, there must exist an edge
f\in E(G_{2})
between
H^{\prime}_{2}(u)
and
H^{\prime}_{2}(v)
. Hence,
H^{\prime}_{1}(u)\cup H^{\prime}_{2}(v)\cup\{f\}
is 2EC.
The remaining case is that
H^{\prime}_{1}
and
H^{\prime}_{2}
are both of type
\mathsf{C}
. In this case,
H^{\prime}_{1}\cup H^{\prime}_{2}
consists of precisely two 2EC components
H^{\prime}(u)
and
H^{\prime}(v)
containing
u
and
v
, respectively.
Since
G
is 2EC, there must exist two edges
f
and
g
between
H^{\prime}(u)
and
H^{\prime}(v)
such that
H^{\prime}_{1}\cup H^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{f,g\}
is 2EC. Thus, in all cases a desired set
F^{\mathsf{C}}
of size at most 2 exists such that
H^{\prime}
is a 2ECSS of
G
.
∎

Lemma 73

(Part of the proof of Lemma A.6 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

2
in
Algorithm

2
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}}
, then
H^{\mathsf{B}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}})
must contain the two dummy edges
wv
and
wu
, which induce a type
\mathsf{B}
graph. Hence, by
Lemma

68
,
H^{\mathsf{B}}_{2}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
or
\mathsf{B}
.
Thus,
H^{\mathsf{B}}=\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}\cup H^{\mathsf{B}}
is a 2ECSS of
G
.
∎

Lemma 74

(Part of the proof of Lemma A.6 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

2
in
Algorithm

2
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}
, then there exists an edge set
F^{\mathsf{C}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{C}}|\leq 1
such that
H^{\mathsf{C}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Note that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}
must be of type
\mathsf{A}
or
\mathsf{B}
.

Lemma

68
guarantees the existence of an edge
f\in E(G_{2})
such that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}\cup\{f\}
is of type
\mathsf{B}
for
G_{1}
. Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}\cup\{f\}
is a 2ECSS of

.
∎

Finally, we prove that the reductions of
Algorithm

2
preserve the approximation factor.

Lemma 75

(Part of the proof of Lemma A.7 in
[
GGJ23
]
)
.

Let
G
be a 2ECSS instance. If every recursive call to
\mathsf{RED}(G^{\prime})
in
Algorithm

2
on input
G
satisfies (
1
), then (
1
) holds for input
G
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{OPT}_{i}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}(G)\cap E(G_{i})
and
\mathsf{opt}_{i}\coloneq\lvert\mathsf{OPT}_{i}\rvert
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Note that
\mathsf{opt}(G)=\mathsf{opt}_{1}+\mathsf{opt}_{2}
.
For the remainder of this proof, all line references (if not specified differently) refer to
Algorithm

2
. We use in every case that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}\geq 2
(cf.
Lemma

69
), and
f(G)\geq 0
by
Proposition

67
.
We distinguish in the following which reduction the algorithm uses.

(
Algorithm

2
:
|V_{1}|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
)

Note that for
i\in\{1,2\}
we have that
\mathsf{OPT}_{i}
is a feasible solution for
G_{i}^{\prime}
and therefore
\mathsf{opt}_{i}\geq\mathsf{opt}(G_{i}^{\prime})
.
Using the assumption of the lemma,
Lemma

72
and
\lvert V(G_{1}^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert V(G_{2}^{\prime})\rvert=\lvert V(G)\rvert
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\prime}_{1}\rvert+\lvert H^{\prime}_{2}\rvert+\lvert F^{\prime}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{1}+\mathsf{opt}_{2})+4\varepsilon(\lvert V(G_{1}^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert V(G_{2}^{\prime})\rvert)-32+2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+4\varepsilon\cdot(\lvert V(G)\rvert)-16=\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

(
Algorithm

2
:
t^{\min}=\mathsf{B}
)

Note that
\mathsf{opt}_{2}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+2
, because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
, which we can assume to be of type
\mathsf{A}
or
\mathsf{B}
by
Lemma

71
and
Lemma

68
, using both dummy edges
\{uw,vw\}
into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\mathsf{B}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}+\lvert H^{\mathsf{B}}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}+(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{2}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}})+f(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B}}))-2

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}+(\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+2)+f(G))-2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(2-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

(
Algorithm

2
:
t^{\min}=\mathsf{C}
)

We distinguish the following cases depending on which type combination of
Lemma

68
is present for
(\mathsf{OPT}_{1},\mathsf{OPT}_{2})
. We exclude the case that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
due to
Lemma

71
. Moreover,
Lemma

74
gives that
|F^{\mathsf{C}}|\leq 1
.

(a)

(\mathsf{B},\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\})
.
In this case,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B}}
exists but
t^{\min}=\mathsf{C}
. Thus, the definition of
Algorithm

2
and
Definition

25
imply that

\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}\leq\mathsf{opt}^{\mathsf{B}}_{1}-1=\mathsf{opt}_{1}-1
. Moreover,
\mathsf{opt}_{2}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+1
, because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edge
\{uv\}
into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}+\lvert H^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{opt}_{1}-1)+(\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+1)+f(G))-1+1

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(1-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

(b)

(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{A})
.
In this case,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}
and
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
is a feasible solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C}}+\lvert H^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}+(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{2}+f(G))-1+1\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

A.2.2
Auxiliary Lemmas for
Algorithm

3

Most of the analysis of
Algorithm

3
is based on
[
GHL24
]
.

Proposition 76
.

If
Algorithm

1
executes
Algorithm

3
, then
G
contains no non-isolating 2-vertex cuts, self loops, parallel edges, irrelevant edges, or
\alpha
-contractible subgraphs with at most
\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
vertices.

Lemma 77
.

Let
G
be the graph when
Algorithm

1
executes
Algorithm

3
,
\{u,v,w\}
be a large 3-vertex cut of
G
,
(V_{1},V_{2})
be partition of
V\setminus\{u,v\}
such that
V_{1}\neq\emptyset\neq V_{2}
and there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, and
H
be a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. Let
G_{i}=G[V_{i}\cup\{u,v,w\}]
and
H_{i}=E(G_{i})\cap H
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then,
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are of a type in
\mathcal{T}_{3}
with respect to
\{u,v,w\}
and
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, respectively.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. First, observe that
H_{i}
consist of either one, two or three connected components, and each component contains at least one of
\{u,v,w\}
, as otherwise
H
would be disconnected. Let
H_{i}^{\prime}
be obtained by contracting each 2EC component
C
of
H_{i}
into a single super-node
C
, and let
C_{i}(x)
be the corresponding super-node of the 2EC component of
H
that contains
x\in V(H_{i})
.

First, assume that
H_{i}
is a single connected component.
In this case
H_{i}^{\prime}
is a tree. If
H_{i}^{\prime}
is composed of a single vertex,
H_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{A}
. Otherwise, let
F
be the set of edges of
H_{i}^{\prime}
that are on any simple path between
C_{i}(u)
,
C_{i}(v)
, and
C_{i}(w)
. If
H_{i}^{\prime}
contains an edge
e\in E(H_{i}^{\prime})\setminus F
, then
e
cannot belong to any cycle of
H
, because it does not belong to a cycle in
H_{i}
and is not on a simple path between any two vertices of the cut
\{u,v,w\}
, contradicting that
H
is 2EC. Thus,
H_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{C1}
if
C_{i}(u)
,
C_{i}(v)
, and
C_{i}(w)
are distinct, and of type
\mathsf{B1}
otherwise.

Second, assume that
H_{i}
is composed of two connected components.
Let
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
and
H_{i}^{\prime}(w)
denote the two trees in
H_{i}^{\prime}
such that w.l.o.g.
u
and
v
are in
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
and
w
is in
H_{i}^{\prime}(w)
.
First note that
H_{i}^{\prime}(w)
must be a single vertex. Otherwise, there is an edge
e
in
H_{i}^{\prime}(w)
that cannot be part of any cycle in
H
, contradicting that
H
is 2EC.
If
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
is also a single vertex, then
H_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{B2}
.
Otherwise, let
F
be the set of edges of
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
on the simple path between
C_{i}(u)
and
C_{i}(v)
.
If
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
contains an edge
e\in E(H_{i}^{\prime}(v))\setminus F
, then
e
cannot belong to any cycle of
H
, contradicting that
H
is 2EC. Thus,
H_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{C2}
.

Finally, assume that
H_{i}
is composed of three connected components.
Let
H_{i}^{\prime}(u)
,
H_{i}^{\prime}(v)
, and
H_{i}^{\prime}(w)
denote the trees of
H_{i}^{\prime}
that contain
u
,
v
, and
w
, respectively.
Using the same argument as before, we can show that each tree is composed of a single vertex, and, thus,
H_{i}
is of type
\mathsf{C3}
.
∎

Lemma 78
.

In
Algorithm

3
it holds that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}\geq\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil\geq 8
.

Proof.

Consider any feasible 2ECSS solution
H_{1}
for
G_{1}
, and let
H^{\prime}_{1}=H_{1}\setminus\{u,v,w\}
be the corresponding solution for
G_{1}|\{u,v,w\}
.
Since
G_{1}|\{u,v,w\}
contains at least
\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil
vertices and
H^{\prime}_{1}
is a feasible 2ECSS solution,
H^{\prime}_{1}
must contain at least
\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil
edges.
Thus,
|H_{1}|\geq|H^{\prime}_{1}|\geq\lceil\frac{2}{\alpha-1}\rceil
.
∎

We have an immediate corollary from the above lemma,
Lemma

66
, and
\alpha\geq\frac{6}{5}
.

Corollary 79
.

If
Algorithm

3
reaches
Algorithm

3
and
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
exists, then
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+3
. In particular,
t^{\min}\neq\mathsf{A}
.

Proof.

\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\frac{6}{5}\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+1\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+\frac{1}{5}8+1\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+\frac{13}{5}

Thus,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+3
.
∎

Lemma 80
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a (multi-)graph that does not contain cut vertices or non-isolating 2-vertex cuts, and let
\{u,v,w\}
be a large 3-vertex cut of
G
.
Let
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a partition of
V\setminus\{u,v,w\}
such that
7\leq\lvert V_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert V_{2}\rvert
and there are no edges between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
in
G
, and let
G_{1}\coloneq G[V_{i}\cup\{u,v,w\}]
and
G_{2}\coloneq G[V_{i}\cup\{u,v,w\}]\setminus\{uv,vw,uw\}
.
For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
H_{i}\subseteq E(G_{i})
, let
H_{i}(z)
be the connected component of
H_{i}
that contains
z\in V(H_{i})
and let
H_{i}
be such that
V(H_{i})=V(H_{i}(u))\cup V(H_{i}(v))\cup V(H_{i}(w))
, i.e., each vertex
y\in V(H_{i})
is connected to
u
,
v
, or
w
in
H_{i}
. Then, the following holds.

1.

For
x\in\{u,v,w\}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, if
H_{i}(x)\neq H_{i}
, then there exists an edge
e\in E(G_{i})\setminus H_{i}
between the vertices of
H_{i}(x)
and the vertices of
H_{i}\setminus H_{i}(x)
.

2.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, if
H_{i}(u)
,
H_{i}(v)
and
H_{i}(w)
are all pairwise vertex-disjoint, then there exist edges
F\subseteq E(G_{i})\setminus H_{i}
such that
|F|\geq 2
and
F\subseteq\{e_{uv},e_{vw},e_{uw}\}
where
e_{xy}
is between the vertices of
H_{i}(x)
and the vertices of
H_{i}(y)
.

Proof.

We prove the first claim, and note that the second claim follows from applying the first claim twice.
Let
i\in\{1,2\}
,
x\in\{u,v,w\}
and
H_{i}(x)\neq H_{i}
.
If
H_{i}(x)
is composed of the single vertex
x
such that there is no edge between
x
and
H_{i}\setminus H_{i}(x)
, then
\{u,v,w\}\setminus\{x\}
must be a non-isolating two-vertex cut in
G
, a contradiction. Otherwise, that is,
H_{i}(x)
consists of at least two vertices, and there is no edge between
H_{i}(x)
and
H_{i}\setminus H_{i}(x)
then
x
is a cut vertex in
G
(separating
V(H_{i}(x))\setminus\{x\}
from
V\setminus V(H_{i}(x))
), a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 81
.

Let
H\subseteq E
be such that
H
is connected and contains a spanning subgraph of
G
, and let
H_{i}\coloneq H\cap E(G_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
If for all
i\in\{1,2\}
every edge
e\in H_{i}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{i}
or lies on an
x-y
path in
H_{i}
where
x,y\in\{u,v,w\}
such that
x\neq y
and there exists an
x-y
path in
H_{j}
where
j=1
if
i=2
and
j=2
if
i=1
, then
H
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

We show that every edge
e\in H
is part of a 2EC component of
H
, which proves the lemma because
H
is connected and spanning.
If for all
i\in\{1,2\}
every edge
e\in H_{i}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{i}
, it is also part of a 2EC component of
H
. Otherwise, for all
i\in\{1,2\}
, if
e
lies on an
x-y
path in
H_{i}
where
x,y\in\{u,v,w\}
such that
x\neq y
and there exists an
x-y
path in
H_{j}
where
j=1
if
i=2
and
j=2
if
i=1
, then
e
is on a cycle of
H
as both paths are edge-disjoint.
∎

Lemma 82
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and both,
H^{\prime}_{1}
and
H^{\prime}_{2}
are 2EC spanning subgraphs of
G^{\prime}_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively, then there exist edges
F^{\prime}
with
|F^{\prime}|\leq 4
such that
H^{\prime}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
, and let
H^{\prime}_{i}(u)
,
H^{\prime}_{i}(v)
, and
H^{\prime}_{i}(w)
be the connected components of
H^{\prime}_{i}
after decontracting
\{u,v,w\}
that contain
u
,
v
and
w
, respectively.
By
Lemma

80
, there exist edges
F_{i}\subseteq E(G_{i})
such that
|F_{i}|\leq 2
and
H^{\prime}_{i}\cup F_{i}
is connected in
G_{i}
.
Since
H^{\prime}_{i}
is spanning in
G_{i}
, setting
F\coloneq F_{1}\cup F_{2}
implies that
|F|\leq 4
and that
H^{\prime}=H^{\prime}_{1}\cup H^{\prime}_{2}\cup F
is spanning and connected in
G
.
Moreover, observe that for
i\in\{1,2\}
each edge
e\in H^{\prime}_{i}\cup F_{i}
is either in a 2EC component of
H^{\prime}_{i}\cup F_{i}
or lies on an
x-y
path in
H^{\prime}_{i}\cup F_{i}
, where
x,y\in\{u,v,w\}
,
x\neq y
. Thus,
Lemma

81
implies that
H^{\prime}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.
∎

Lemma 83
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{B1}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{B1}}|\leq 1
such that
H^{\mathsf{B1}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Assume w.l.o.g. that
u
and
v
belong to the same 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}
.
Let
H_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}
and
C_{i}^{\mathsf{B1}}(x)
be the 2EC component of
H_{i}^{\mathsf{B1}}
that contains
x
, where
x\in\{u,v,w\}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Since
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)=C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(v)
, we have that in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}
, the vertices of
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)
,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)
, and
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}(v)
are in the same 2EC component.
Furthermore, in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}
, there is a connection between
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)
and
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(w)
.
If
w
and
v
are already connected in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}
then we can set
F^{\mathsf{B1}}\coloneq\emptyset
and see that this satisfies the lemma, since
H^{\mathsf{B1}}
contains a spanning tree and every edge in
H^{\mathsf{B1}}
is either in the 2EC component containing
u
and
v
, in a 2EC component containing
w
or on a
w-v
path in
G_{i}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Otherwise, if
w
and
v
are not in the same connected component in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}
, by
Lemma

80
, there must be an edge
e
from
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}(w)
to either
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}(v)
or
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)
in
E(G_{2})\setminus H^{\mathsf{B1}}
.
Now it can be easily verified that
F^{\mathsf{B1}}\coloneq\{e\}
satisfies the lemma, since
H^{\mathsf{B1}}
contains a spanning tree and every edge in
H^{\mathsf{B1}}
is either in the 2EC component containing
u
and
v
, in a 2EC component containing
w
or on a
w-v
path in
G_{i}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
.
∎

Lemma 84
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B2}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{B2}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{B2}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{B2}}|\leq 2
such that
H^{\mathsf{B2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the previous one.
Assume w.l.o.g. that
u
and
v
belong to the same 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}
.
Let
H_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}
and
C_{i}^{\mathsf{B2}}(x)
be the 2EC component of
H_{i}^{\mathsf{B2}}
that contains
x
, where
x\in\{u,v,w\}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Since
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}(u)=C_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}(v)
, we have that in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{B2}}
, the vertices of
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}(u)
,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B2}}(u)
and
C_{2}^{\mathsf{B2}}(v)
are in the same 2EC component.
Furthermore, in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}
, there is no connection between
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}(u)
and
C_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}(w)
, since it is of type
\mathsf{B2}
.
Hence, by
Lemma

80
, there must be an edge
e_{1}\in E(G_{1})\setminus\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}
.
Note that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B2}}\cup\{e_{1}\}
is now a solution of type
\mathsf{B1}
for
G_{1}
, and hence we can do the same as in the proof of the previous lemma and observe that
\lvert F^{\mathsf{B2}}\rvert\leq 2
.
∎

Lemma 85
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{C1}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{C1}}|\leq 2
such that
H^{\mathsf{C1}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Let
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}(x)
be the 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
that contains
x
, for
x\in\{u,v,w\}
.
Note that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is connected and spanning for
G_{1}
and that each edge
e\in\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is either in some 2EC component or it is on a
y-z
-path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}
for
y,z\in\{u,v,w\}
,
y\neq z
.
We consider three cases.
If
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is connected, then we claim that
F^{\mathsf{C1}}\coloneq\emptyset
satisfies the lemma.
In this case every edge of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
, or it is on a
y-z
-path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
for
y,z\in\{u,v,w\}
,
y\neq z
.
Hence,
Lemma

81
implies that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Next, assume that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is composed of two connected components, say w.l.o.g. one containing
u
and
v
and the other containing
w
, and observe that each component is 2EC. Then
Lemma

80
guarantees the existence of an edge
e
in
G_{2}
between
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}(w)
and
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}(u)
such that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Thus, we can analogously to the previous case show that

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C1}}\coloneq\{e\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, since in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
each edge is either in some 2EC component or it is on a
y-z
-path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
for
y,z\in\{u,v,w\}
,
y\neq z
(hence
Lemma

81
holds).
In the remaining case
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
is composed of three connected components, each containing exactly one vertex of
\{u,v,w\}
.
Observe that each component is 2EC.
Hence,
Lemma

80
guarantees the existence of at least two edges
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
in
G_{2}
between two pairs of connected components of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}
such that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
is connected.
Thus, we can analogously to the previous cases show that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C1}}\coloneq\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, since in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
each edge is either in some 2EC component or it is on a
y-z
-path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C1}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C1}}
for
y,z\in\{u,v,w\}
,
y\neq z
(hence
Lemma

81
holds).
∎

Lemma 86
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|\leq 1
such that
H^{\mathsf{C2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
and
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|\leq-2
.

Proof.

Fix an optimal
\mathsf{C2}
solution
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
for
G_{1}
. Note that both dummy edges
uy
and
vy
must be part of
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
as otherwise
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
. By the same reason,
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
must be connected and spanning for
G_{2}
.

We first consider the case where
vw\in\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
.
Let
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
be the 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that contains
w
. Since
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
is isolated in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
,
Lemma

80
implies that there exists an edge
e
in
G_{1}
between
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
and
V(G_{1})\setminus C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
.
Then,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
is connected and spanning for
G_{1}
, and every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Similarly, every edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
.
Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C2}}\coloneq\{e\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
by
Lemma

81
. Moreover,
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=1-3=-2
.

If
vw\notin\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
, then every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between
u
and
v
between which is also a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Further, every edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between
u
and
v
, and there is a path between
u
and
v
in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Note that there cannot be an edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that is on a path between
w
and
u
or
v
but not in a 2EC component, because then
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
as
vw\notin\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
.
Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
by
Lemma

81
.
We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=0-2=-2
.
∎

Lemma 87
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|\leq 1
and
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
such that
H^{\mathsf{C2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
and
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|\leq-3
.

Proof.

Let
D=\{uy,vz,zy,wy\}
.
First observe that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
must be connected and spanning for
G_{2}
.
Note that
vz
and
zy
must be part of
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
to make
z
incident to 2 edges, and additionally
vy
or
wy
must be part of
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
to make
y
incident to at least 2 edges.
Thus, we can distinguish the following cases.

If
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\cap D=\{uy,vz,zy\}
(or by symmetry
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\cap D=\{wy,vz,zy\}
), let
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
be an optimal
\mathsf{C2}
solution for
G_{1}
that contains a
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(u)-C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(v)
path, where
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(x)
denotes the 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that contains
x
, for
x\in\{u,v,w\}
.
Note that every edge of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between
u
and
v
between which is also a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Further, every edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between
u
and
v
, and there is a path between
u
and
v
in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
. Note that there cannot be an edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that is on a path between
w
and
u
or
v
but not in a 2EC component, because then
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
as
wy\notin\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
.
Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
by
Lemma

81
. We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=0-3=-3
.

Otherwise, that is,
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\cap D=\{vy,vz,zy,wy\}
,
let
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
be an optimal
\mathsf{C2}
solution for
G_{1}
that contains a
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(u)-C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(v)
path of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
, where
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(x)
denotes the 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that contains
x
, for
x\in\{u,v,w\}
.

Lemma

80
implies that there exists an edge
e
in
G_{1}
between
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
and the connected component containing
V(G_{1})\setminus V(C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w))
. Therefore,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Thus, every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(resp.
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
(
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
).
We conclude using
Lemma

81
that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C2}}\coloneq\{e\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=1-4=-3
.
∎

Lemma 88
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with

and
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
such that

is a 2EC spanning subgraph of

and

.

.

.

\mathsf{C2}

denotes the 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that contains
x
, for

.

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
between
u
and
v
between which is also a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Further, every edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is part of a 2EC component of

between

and

, and there is a path between

and

in

. Note that there cannot be an edge in

that is on a path between
w
and

or

but not in a 2EC component, because then
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
as

.
Thus,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
by
Lemma

81
.
We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=0-2=-2
.

Otherwise, that is,
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\cap D=\{uy,vy,wy\}
,
let
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
be an optimal
\mathsf{C2}
solution for
G_{1}
that contains a
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(u)-C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(v)
path, where
C_{i}^{\mathsf{C2}}(x)
denotes the 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
that contains
x
, for
x\in\{u,v,w\}
.

Lemma

80
implies that there exists an edge
e
in
G_{1}
between
C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w)
and the connected component containing
V(G_{1})\setminus V(C_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}(w))
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Thus, every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(resp.
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
) between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
(
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\{e\}
).
We conclude using
Lemma

81
that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C2}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C2}}\coloneq\{e\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C2}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}|=1-3=-2
.
∎

Lemma 89
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

3
in
Algorithm

3
applies and
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
, then there exist edges
F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|\leq 4
such that
H^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|\leq 0
.

Proof.

First note that the claimed
\mathsf{C3}
solution in
Algorithm

3
is guaranteed by
Lemma

80
(among renaming
u
,
v
and
w
).
Let
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(x)
be the 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
that contains
x
, for
x\in\{u,v,w\}
.
We have 4 main cases.

Case 1:

|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{uv,vw\}|=\ell\leq 2
. First observe that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is connected as otherwise
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
. Moreover, we can select
\ell
edges
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\subseteq\{e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
such that

\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is connected.
Further, note that every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(resp.
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
).
Thus,
Lemma

81
implies that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.
We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=\ell-\ell=0
.

Case 2:

|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{uv,uv\}|=2
(the case
|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{vw,vw\}|=2
is symmetric).
We have that
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)=C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
, as otherwise
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})
cannot be feasible for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
.
If
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is 2EC, then clearly
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=0-2\leq 0
.

If
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
is connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
, then
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is connected.
Further,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv}\}
is connected.
Every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
between
u
and
v
, which are also connected in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
. Note that there cannot be an edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
on a path between
w
and
u
or
v
that is not in a 2EC component by the choice of the
\mathsf{C3}
solution
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}
.
Moreover, every edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is part of a 2EC component of
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
or lies on a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
between
u
and
v
or
w
, which are also connected in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
. Note that there cannot be an edge in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
on a path between
v
and
w
that is not in a 2EC component, because
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)=C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
is 2EC.
Thus,
Lemma

81
implies that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=1-2\leq 0
.

Otherwise, that is,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
is not connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
,
by
Lemma

80
there must exist an edge
e
in
G_{2}
between
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
and
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
such that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Thus, we can analogously to the previous case show that

H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e,e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv}\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.
We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=2-2=0
.

Case 3:

|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{uv,uv,vw\}|=3
(the case
|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{uv,vw,vw\}|=3
is symmetric).
Note that in this case
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
is connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
as otherwise
u
and
w
cannot be 2EC in
\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})
.
We have two cases.

If
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
is connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
, then
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is connected.
Further,
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
is connected and spanning for
G_{1}
.
Thus, every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(resp.
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
).
We conclude via
Lemma

81
that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=2-3\leq 0
.

Otherwise, that is,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
is not connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
,
Lemma

80
guarantees the existence of an edge
e
in
G_{2}
between the vertices of
V(C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w))
and the vertices of
V(H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\setminus V(C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w))
.
Therefore,
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Hence, we can analogously to the previous case show that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e,e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=3-3=0
.

Case 4:

|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\cap\{uv,uv,vw,vw\}|=4
. We distinguish three cases.

First, assume that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is connected. We have that
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
is connected and spanning for
G_{1}
.
Thus, every edge in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(resp.
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) is part of a 2EC component of
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) or lies on a path in
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
) between any two distinct vertices of
\{u,v,w\}
between which is a path in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
(
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
).
We conclude via
Lemma

81
that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=2-4\leq 0
.

If
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is composed of two connected components, assume w.l.o.g. that
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
is connected to
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
and the other connected component in
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}
is
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
.
In this case,
Lemma

80
guarantees that there is an edge
e
in
E(G_{2})\setminus C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
between the vertices of
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
and the vertices of
V(H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})\setminus V(C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w))
.
Therefore,
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\{e\}
is connected.
Hence, we can analogously to the previous case show that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}
with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq\{e,e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
, and
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=3-4\leq 0
.

Otherwise, that is,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(u)
,
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(v)
and
C_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}(w)
are all pairwise disjoint,
Lemma

80
guarantees the existence of two edges
F\subseteq E(G_{2})
such that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F
is connected.
Hence, we can analogously to the previous case(s) show that
H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}\cup F^{\mathsf{C3}}

with
F^{\mathsf{C3}}\coloneq F\cup\{e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{uv},e^{\mathsf{C3}}_{vw}\}
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
. We have that
|F^{\mathsf{C3}}|-|\mathsf{RED}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})|+|H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}|=4-4=0
.
∎

Finally, we prove that the reductions of
Algorithm

3
preserve the approximation factor.
While the proof is quite long, it is of the same spirit of the analogue for
Algorithm

2
(cf.
Lemma

75
) and easy to verify: We consider different cases depending on the reduction the algorithm uses and, if
Algorithm

3
is executed, we distinguish for some cases further which solution types the actual optimal solution uses for both sides of the cut. For each case, we can easily derive bounds for the different parts of the solution that our algorithm produces, and put them together to obtain the overall bound, which is stated below.

Lemma 90
.

Let
G
be a 2ECSS instance. If every recursive call to
\mathsf{RED}(G^{\prime})
in
Algorithm

3
on input
G
satisfies (
1
), then (
1
) holds for input
G
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{OPT}_{i}\coloneq\mathsf{OPT}(G)\cap E(G_{i})
and
\mathsf{opt}_{i}\coloneq\lvert\mathsf{OPT}_{i}\rvert
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Note that
\mathsf{opt}(G)=\mathsf{opt}_{1}+\mathsf{opt}_{2}
.
For the remainder of this proof, all line references (if not specified differently) refer to
Algorithm

3
. Note that
Lemma

78
gives
\mathsf{opt}_{1}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}\geq\frac{2}{\alpha-1}
. Furthermore, throughout the proof we require
\alpha\geq\frac{6}{5}
. We also use that
f(G)\geq 0
, because
|V(G)|\geq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
by
Proposition

76
. We distinguish in the following which reduction the algorithm uses.

(
Algorithm

3
:
|V_{1}|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
)

Note that for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have that
\mathsf{OPT}_{i}
is a feasible solution for
G_{i}^{\prime}
and therefore
\mathsf{opt}_{i}\geq\mathsf{opt}(G_{i}^{\prime})
.
Using the induction hypothesis,
Lemma

82
and
\lvert V(G_{1})\rvert+\lvert V(G_{2})\rvert=\lvert V_{1}\rvert+\lvert V_{2}\rvert+2\leq\lvert V(G)\rvert
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle=\lvert H^{\prime}_{1}\rvert+\lvert H^{\prime}_{2}\rvert+\lvert F^{\prime}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{1}+\mathsf{opt}_{2})+4\varepsilon(\lvert V_{1}\rvert+\lvert V_{2}\rvert+2)-32+4

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+4\varepsilon\cdot(\lvert V(G)\rvert)-16=\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

(
Algorithm

3
:
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}
exists and
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+1
)

Note that
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}
, because
u
,
v
, and
w
are contracted into a single vertex in
G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}
.
Thus,
\mathsf{opt}(G)=\mathsf{opt}_{2}+\mathsf{opt}_{1}\geq\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}})+\mathsf{opt}_{1}
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+1\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}+1
, and
\lvert F^{\mathsf{B1}}\rvert\leq 1
by
Lemma

83
. Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\mathsf{B1}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}+\lvert H_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{B1}}\rvert\leq(\mathsf{opt}_{1}+1)+(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{2}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{B1}})+f(G))+1

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)-(\alpha-1)\mathsf{opt}_{1}+2\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ ,

where we use
\mathsf{opt}_{1}\geq\frac{2}{\alpha-1}
.

(
Algorithms

3
and
3
:
t^{\min}=\mathsf{B2}
or
t^{\min}=\mathsf{C1}
)

Let
\tau\coloneq t^{\min}
.
Note that
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\tau})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}
, because
u
,
v
, and
w
are contracted into a single vertex in
G_{2}^{\tau}
. Thus,
\mathsf{opt}(G)\geq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+\mathsf{opt}_{1}(G_{1}^{\tau})\geq\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\tau})+\mathsf{opt}_{1}(G_{1}^{\tau})
. Moreover,
\lvert F^{\tau}\rvert\leq 2
by
Lemmas

84
and
85
. Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=\lvert H^{\tau}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\tau}+\lvert H_{2}^{\tau}\rvert+\lvert F^{\tau}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}^{\tau}_{1}+(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}_{2}(G_{2}^{\tau})+f(G))+2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)-(\alpha-1)\mathsf{opt}^{\tau}_{1}+2\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ ,

where we use
\mathsf{opt}_{1}\geq\frac{2}{\alpha-1}
.

(
Algorithm

3
:
t^{\min}=\mathsf{C2}
)

In
Algorithm

3
we have the following
\mathsf{C2}
subtypes, which use different reductions
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
for the recursion (
Algorithms

3
,
3
and
3
).

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(i)}
(
Algorithm

3
):

Every
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
solution contains a
C(u)-C(v)
path.
In this case
the definition of
Algorithm

3
,
Lemma

86
, and the induction hypothesis gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=|H^{\mathsf{C2}}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}|+\lvert H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\mathsf{red}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})-2

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})+f(G)-2\ .

(2)

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(ii)}
(
Algorithm

3
):

Every
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
solution contains either a
C(u)-C(v)
path or a
C(v)-C(w)
path.
In this case
the definition of
Algorithm

3
,
Lemma

87
, and the induction hypothesis gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=|H^{\mathsf{C2}}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}|+\lvert H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\mathsf{red}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})-3

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})+f(G)-3\ .

(3)

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(iii)}
(
Algorithm

3
):

Every
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}
solution contains some path between
C(u)
,
C(v)
, and
C(w)
.
In this case
the definition of
Algorithm

3
,
Lemma

88
, and the induction hypothesis gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)=|H^{\mathsf{C2}}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}|+\lvert H_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C2}}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\mathsf{red}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})-2

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})+f(G)-2\ .

(4)

We distinguish the following cases depending on which type combination of
Proposition

62
is present for
(\mathsf{OPT}_{1},\mathsf{OPT}_{2})
.

(a)

(\mathsf{A},\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2},\mathsf{C1},\mathsf{C2},\mathsf{C3}\})
. Since
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}
exists,
Corollary

79
gives
\mathsf{opt}_{1}=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{A}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+3
.

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(i)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vy,vw\}
and at most two more edges, which are guaranteed by

80
if required, into a solution for

. Thus, (

) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{opt}_{1}-3)+(\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5)+f(G)-2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(5-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(ii)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+6
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vz,zy,wy\}
and at most two more edges, which are guaranteed by

if required, into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
. Thus, (
3
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{opt}_{1}-3)+(\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+6)+f(G)-3

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(6-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(iii)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vy,wy\}
and at most two more edges, which are guaranteed by
Lemma

80
if required, into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
. Thus, (
4
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{opt}_{1}-3)+(\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5)+f(G)-2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(5-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

(b)

(\mathsf{B1},\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B1},\mathsf{B2},\mathsf{C1},\mathsf{C2}\})
.
Since
\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}
exists but the condition in

did not apply, we conclude that
\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{B1}}\geq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\min}+2=\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C2}}+2
.

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(i)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+4
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vy,vw\}
and at most one more edge, which is guaranteed by

if required, into a solution for

.
Thus, continuing (
2
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}-2+\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+4)+f(G)-2

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(4-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(ii)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vz,zy,wy\}
and at most one more edge, which is guaranteed by
Lemma

80
if required, into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Thus, (
3
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}-2+\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+5)+f(G)-3

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)+(\alpha-1)(5-\mathsf{opt}_{1})\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ .

Subtype
\mathsf{C2(iii)}
:

We have
\mathsf{opt}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}})\leq\mathsf{opt}_{2}+4
because we can turn
\mathsf{OPT}_{2}
with the dummy edges
\{uy,vy,wy\}
and at most one more edge, which is guaranteed by
Lemma

80
if required, into a solution for
G_{2}^{\mathsf{C2}}
.
Thus, (
4
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}-2+\alpha\cdot(\mathsf{opt}_{2}+4)+f(G)-2

\displaystyle\leq|\mathsf{OPT}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}|+\lvert H_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}}\rvert+\lvert F^{\mathsf{C3}}\rvert\leq\mathsf{opt}_{1}^{\mathsf{C3}}+\mathsf{red}(G_{2}^{\mathsf{C3}})

(5)

where we use

A.2.3
Auxiliary Lemmas for
Algorithm

4

Proposition 91
.

If
Algorithm

1
executes
Algorithm

4
, then
|V(G)|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 92
.

In
Algorithm

4
, it holds that
\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})\geq\lceil\frac{k}{\alpha-1}\rceil
.

Proof.

Since
G^{\prime}_{1}=G_{1}|\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
contains at least
|V_{1}|+1\geq(\lceil\frac{k}{\alpha-1}\rceil-1)+1
vertices and
\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})
is a feasible 2ECSS solution for
G^{\prime}_{1}
,
\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})
must contain at least
\lceil\frac{k}{\alpha-1}\rceil
edges.
∎

Lemma 93
.

In
Algorithm

4
, if both
H_{1}=\mathsf{RED}(G^{\prime}_{1})
and
H_{2}=\mathsf{RED}(G^{\prime}_{2})
are 2EC spanning subgraphs of
G^{\prime}_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively, then
H
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.

Proof.

Note that both
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
and hence both
G^{\prime}_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{2}
must be 2EC since
G
is 2EC.
By assumption
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are 2EC spanning subgraphs of
G^{\prime}_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively. Thus, their union is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G|\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
. Since
H
additionally to
H_{1}\cup H_{2}
contains a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G[\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}]
, we conclude that
H
is a 2EC spanning subgraph of
G
.
∎

Lemma 94
.

Let
G
be a 2ECSS instance. If every recursive call to
\mathsf{RED}(G^{\prime})
in
Algorithm

4
on input
G
satisfies (
1
), then (
1
) holds for input
G
.

Proof.

Assume
|V_{1}|\leq|V_{2}|
. Note that
\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})+\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{2})\leq\mathsf{opt}(G)
and
|V(G^{\prime}_{1})|+|V(G^{\prime}_{2})|\leq|V(G)|
.
By
Proposition

91
, we have
|V(G)|\geq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
. Thus, we need to show that

\mathsf{red}(G)\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)=\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G)|-16\ .

We distinguish three cases.

(
|V_{2}|\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
and
|V_{1}|\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
).

By (
1
), we have
H_{i}=\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{i})
for all
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then

\mathsf{red}(G)=|H_{1}|+|H_{2}|+k=\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})+\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{2})+k\leq\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G)|-16=\alpha\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ ,

because
\alpha\geq 1
and
|V(G)|\geq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
gives
k\leq 16\leq 4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G)|-16
.

(
|V_{2}|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
and
|V_{1}|\leq\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
).

By (
1
), we have
H_{1}=\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})
and
|H_{2}|\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{2})+f(G^{\prime}_{2})
. Then,

\mathsf{red}(G)=|H_{1}|+|H_{2}|+k\leq\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})+\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{2})+f(G^{\prime}_{2})+k\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ ,

where we use that
f(G^{\prime}_{2})\leq f(G)
and
\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})\geq\frac{k}{\alpha-1}
(
Lemma

92
).

(
|V_{2}|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
and
|V_{1}|>\frac{8}{\varepsilon}
).

By (
1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{red}(G)

\displaystyle=|H_{1}|+|H_{2}|+k

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{1})+4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G^{\prime}_{1})|-16+\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G^{\prime}_{2})+4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G^{\prime}_{2})|-16+k

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+4\varepsilon\cdot|V(G)|-16=\alpha\cdot\mathsf{opt}(G)+f(G)\ ,

because
k\leq 8
.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proposition 95
.

If

1
executes

5
for

, then

, and

-vertex cuts.

Lemma 96
.

If the condition in
Algorithm

5
in

5
holds, we have

.

Proof.

Since
G^{\prime}_{1}=G_{1}|\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
contains at least

vertices and

,
